# Decisions

| ID | Date | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|---|
| D001 | 2026-09-16 | Drop SRE/NOC as the product. Skanyxx is a licensed **agent management workspace**. | Customer value is building agents, skills, MCP, and company knowledge — not watching incidents. |
| D002 | 2026-09-16 | **KAgent stays the runtime** that connects the customer’s models to agents. Skanyxx does not replace kagent. | That path already works; kagent is the Kubernetes-native agent platform ([docs](https://kagent.dev/docs/kagent/)). |
| D003 | 2026-09-16 | **One install per customer.** | Isolation of people, models, agents, and memory. Not a shared multi-tenant cloud. |
| D004 | 2026-09-16 | **One UI, three roles** (owner, builder, employee). Any of them can be the first user. | Same product; home/permissions change with role. Not three apps. |
| D005 | 2026-09-16 | Community of templates is **planned and urgent**, but shares **recipes only** (agent / skill / MCP). Never customer memory. | Need a catalog path soon; company knowledge is the customer’s IP. |
| D006 | 2026-09-16 | Company memory is a **governed engine**, not “record into one agent.” Intake can be any work (chat, files, meetings, tickets, voice, agent runs). | Recording was only an example. Knowledge must be reusable across relevant agents. |
| D007 | 2026-09-16 | People contribute to **collections**; builders **grant collections to agents**. | Aggregation without dumping the whole company brain into every bot. |
| D008 | 2026-09-16 | Do **not** treat kagent’s built-in agent memory as the company memory bank. | kagent memory is per-agent, TTL (default 15 days), **no cross-agent sharing** ([agent memory](https://kagent.dev/docs/kagent/concepts/agent-memory/)). kagent itself says a company store should come in as **MCP**. |
| D009 | 2026-09-16 | People can **search and open** memory items in Skanyxx. This is a knowledge library, **not a file explorer**. Daily use is still mostly through agents. | Later someone must read *what* we did and *why* without guessing. A folder tree of files is the wrong metaphor. |
| D010 | 2026-09-16 | Agents **may write** into company memory when they have that skill and collection grant. They write structured items (decision / fact / open), not raw chat logs. | Example: this design thread. If Skanyxx-agent had been the partner, the bank would already hold D001–D011 so a future person (or agent) can recover the trail. |
| D011 | 2026-09-16 | Publish policy is **per collection** (and by agent trust). No single global “always review” or “always auto”. | Legal/HR may need a queue. A trusted design-agent writing to `skanyxx-design` may auto-publish. |
| D012 | 2026-09-16 | **Sessions are not memory.** Chat/work logs are ephemeral (or a separate audit store with short TTL). The bank only keeps extracted items. | Logging every employee×agent turn would explode storage. Memory is “what/why we locked,” not the conversation. |
| D013 | 2026-09-16 | Agents **never read the bank**. They **search** granted collections and get **top-k** small cards into context. | Re-feeding stored text every turn would burn tokens and still miss the point. Retrieval, not reread. |
| D014 | 2026-09-16 | Three layers: **card** (tiny, for search+prompt), **body** (optional, for a human opening the item), **source** (blob/URL, never inlined into the agent). | Recordings, PDFs, transcripts stay cheap object storage. Prompts only see the card. |
| D015 | 2026-09-16 | Same fact is **updated or superseded**, not appended forever. | Stops duplicate “we use kagent” items from filling the index and the prompt. |
| D016 | 2026-09-16 | Three planes: **Skanyxx** = workspace, **kagent** = runtime, **memory engine** = company knowledge via MCP. | Mixing them causes a fake SRE console, token reread, or treating kagent TTL memory as the company brain. See `three-planes.md`. |
| D017 | 2026-09-16 | People talk to agents **only in Skanyxx chat** (day one). Slack / IDE / kagent UI are not the conversation surface. | Chat is the product. kagent UI stays an ops detail of the runtime. |
| D018 | 2026-09-16 | The license **includes our setup**: we install Skanyxx + kagent (+ memory) on the customer’s side. They do not bring their own kagent. | One working stack per customer. Owner should not assemble the three planes by hand. |
| D019 | 2026-09-16 | **Start clean.** Do not ship kagent’s sample agent pack. Ship **one general-purpose agent** wired to the configured model + memory MCP + `company` collection. It is the seed of the ecosystem, not a fleet. | Production should not look like a demo cluster. One talker starts filling memory so later (studio) agents have soil. |
| D020 | 2026-09-16 | Factory is **builder sugar**: propose kagent YAML via chat. It becomes live **only after a git PR is merged**. Studio edits the same files. | Agents are YAML. PR is the approval mechanism. Prevents a silent fleet from one chat. |
| D021 | 2026-09-16 | **Memory cards never use PRs.** Only agent/MCP/skill/grant definitions do. | Card volume would spam git and is a different lifecycle (D012). |
| D022 | 2026-09-16 | Factory tools are for **builder/dev roles**, not employees talking to the seed agent. | Employees grow knowledge. Builders (with review) grow the fleet. |
| D023 | 2026-09-16 | **We create** the agent-config repo as part of setup. If they give GitHub/GitLab, create it there. If not, bundle git in the stack. Pointing at an existing repo is an **owner option later**, not day one. | First hour cannot depend on a GitOps shop already existing. Real orgs can attach their monorepo later. |
| D024 | 2026-09-16 | **Agent supervisor** merges agent PRs (the approval). Builders open PRs; they cannot make an agent live alone. Small orgs: owner also wears supervisor. | Dedicated responsibility, not “any builder” and not “only the CEO title”. |
| D025 | 2026-09-16 | **First login is the owner** created by setup. They configure (model, git if needed) and **add users**. Nobody else exists until they do. | One named person must jump in. Chat and cards need identity. |
| D026 | 2026-09-16 | Adding users: **invite always works**. Optional directory (**Entra ID** first) after first login — not a setup blocker. | Many customers have Entra; many do not. First hour cannot wait on SSO. |
| D027 | 2026-09-16 | Directory does **not** dump the whole tenant in. Owner maps IdP **groups → Skanyxx roles**. Unmapped people have no access. | A company memory bank is not a public Slack. Accidental 10k employees is worse than a slow invite. |
| D028 | 2026-09-16 | **Studio** is a composer form over the agent-config git repo. Save opens a PR. It is not live-apply and not kagent’s UI. | Same source of truth as factory (D020). Supervisor still the gate (D024). |
| D029 | 2026-09-16 | An agent in studio is: **name + model + instructions + skills + MCP tools + collection grants** (+ optional kagent TTL memory). | That is the kagent Agent CR plus Skanyxx grants. Community templates later fill these slots, they are not extra slots. |
| D030 | 2026-09-16 | Builders (and supervisor) get **Preview chat** on the PR/branch sandbox. | Otherwise every prompt typo needs a merge. |
| D031 | 2026-09-16 | **Employees** only see **merged** agents in Skanyxx chat. Drafts are not in the company fleet. | Supervisor owns what the company talks to. |
| D032 | 2026-09-16 | Preview may **search** production collections; it must **not upsert** cards into them. | A draft agent must not pollute the company bank. |
| D033 | 2026-09-16 | Per collection, two grants: **search** and **upsert**. An agent can have search without upsert. | A FAQ agent may read `billing` and must not write it. |
| D034 | 2026-09-16 | A memory **card** is: `collection`+`key`, `type`, `what`, `why`, `who`, `when`, `status`; `body` and `source` stay off the default prompt. | Tiny on purpose. See `memory-card.md`. |
| D035 | 2026-09-16 | Upsert identity is **`collection` + `key`** (e.g. `billing/refund-window`). Same key overwrites; it does not append. | D015 needs a name, not fuzzy “looks similar.” |
| D036 | 2026-09-16 | Card types are only **decision, fact, procedure, open**. | More types become a dumping ground. Media is `source`, not a type. |
| D037 | 2026-09-16 | Hard caps: `what` 200 chars, `why` 400 chars. Default retrieve **top-5 published** cards. | Token budget is a product rule, not a hope. |
| D038 | 2026-09-16 | Agent **proposes** `key`; a human may rename it in the library. | Slugs must be machine-usable and still fixable. |
| D039 | 2026-09-16 | Day one only one collection: **`company`**. No per-user `private` yet. | Clean start. More collections when a builder/supervisor adds them. |
| D040 | 2026-09-16 | New agents default to **search** on `company`, **not upsert**. Write is an explicit grant. | 100 agents must not all stamp the bank. Storm is a permission problem first. |
| D041 | 2026-09-16 | Writes go through the **memory engine**, serialized per `collection/key`, with a **version**. Stale version = conflict (agent re-reads). No silent last-write-wins. | Two agents editing the same fact must not clobber without noticing. |
| D042 | 2026-09-16 | **Do not orchestrate agents with Redis/Dragonfly.** Optional later: cache + distributed lock **inside** the memory service. Source of truth stays the memory store. | Redis is not a fleet brain. kagent runs agents; the bank coordinates **keys**. |
| D043 | 2026-09-16 | When the memory service needs a cache/lock (multi-replica, hot reads), use **Dragonfly**. | Named later; not in the first-hour stack. Same role as D042’s “optional.” |
| D044 | 2026-09-16 | Chat **transcripts** default TTL **14 days** (owner-configurable). Memory **cards** are not on that TTL. | Sessions are not the bank (D012). Cards live until superseded/policy. |
| D045 | 2026-09-16 | Merged agent YAML is applied by **our reconciler** to kagent. Flux/Argo may replace that later. | We provide the setup (D018). Day one is not “please install Argo.” |
| D046 | 2026-09-16 | Company memory is a **Skanyxx engine** (MCP to kagent). **Not Joicy** as the store. | Joicy is code/git-shaped; we need collections, version, grants. Keep Joicy as sister. |
| D047 | 2026-09-16 | Production setup target is the **customer’s Kubernetes** (Helm/installer deploys the three planes + git). | kagent is k8s-native. One stack per customer. |
| D048 | 2026-09-16 | Day-one **library**: logged-in users can open **published `company`** cards. That is independent of agent grants. | Humans audit the trail (D009). Agents still need search/upsert (D033). |
| D049 | 2026-09-16 | Memory scopes: **personal → team → department → company**. D039 (only `company`) is superseded. | Knowledge is born in private chats; the product is **lift**, not one pile. See `memory-hierarchy.md`. |
| D050 | 2026-09-16 | Chat history stays **personal**. We lift **cards**, never the transcript, to team/dept/company. | Same volume rule as D012. Fix silos without cloning every DM. |
| D051 | 2026-09-16 | Promote **copies up**. The author’s personal card remains. | Higher layer can supersede without erasing origin. |
| D052 | 2026-09-16 | Seed/employee **upsert defaults to personal**. Company search stays the default grant for new agents. Lift into **company** needs supervisor/owner; into **team** needs a team member. | Stops 100 agents publishing maniacally to the company brain. |
| D053 | 2026-09-16 | Memory stack: **Postgres** (cards, sessions, FTS, versions) + **S3/MinIO** (blobs) + MCP service. **Dragonfly** later. **pgvector** later. Not Joicy. | Durable source of truth on k8s. See `memory-stack.md`. |
| D054 | 2026-09-16 | **Community catalog paused.** | Memory hierarchy and stack first. |
| D055 | 2026-09-16 | **Org tree lives in Skanyxx** (people, teams, departments). Entra/Google/Okta are optional **mappers**, not required. No Azure → owner builds the tree by invite. | Hierarchy cannot wait on SSO. Azure is one customer, not the product. |
| D056 | 2026-09-16 | License → owner runs **our installer once**. UI is the **browser URL**. Invited users open that URL. They do not install per-user apps. | Chat is a website. See `install-path.md`. |
| D057 | 2026-09-16 | Two install modes: **appliance** (Linux VM / marketplace; we embed k3s, they never see k8s) and **bring-your-cluster** (Helm). Same workloads. | kagent needs k8s. Most buyers should not have to know what that is. |
| D058 | 2026-09-16 | **Skanyxx (UI + control plane) is separate from kagent.** It **configures controller URLs** (kagent, memory MCP) as today. Default appliance/Helm colocates them. Split is supported. | Matches current Skanyxx. k8s is for the runtime, not a requirement for where the browser app runs. |
| D059 | 2026-09-16 | A **downloadable desktop client** is allowed (UI like today). It calls **shared** kagent + **shared** Skanyxx/memory URLs. It must **not** keep the company user DB, org tree, or lifted cards only on that laptop. | Today’s one-operator local backend does not scale to a company bank. Client vs source of truth. |
| D060 | 2026-09-16 | The **product we ship** is a **Helm umbrella** (Skanyxx API, kagent, memory, Postgres, MinIO, git). Today’s fat local Skanyxx **thins** to a browser/desktop **client** of that umbrella. | Company brain is the cluster chart. The Windows build is not the source of truth. |
| D061 | 2026-09-16 | Umbrella **bundles** Postgres, MinIO, and git **on by default**. Helm values can **turn each off** and point at the customer’s own (RDS/Cloud SQL, S3/Azure Blob, GitHub/GitLab). | Appliance works with zero extra accounts. Platform teams keep their existing data stores. |
| D062 | 2026-09-25 | **Do not support Google AX** as a second runtime. kagent remains the only controller (D002). Revisit only if a customer already standardizes on AX. | AX is a sandbox **task** orchestrator (Substrate + Redis), still unstable. Dual runtimes would fork studio/factory/Helm. |
| D063 | 2026-09-25 | **Supersedes D062.** v1 includes AX as a **Tasks** feature, not a second Agents runtime. kagent remains the **Agents** experience (D002). No “AX but chat like kagent.” | Same UI, two jobs. See `ax-tasks.md`. |
| D064 | 2026-09-25 | AX in v1 is **thin and Experimental**: connect, list, run/watch/stop, attach **our memory MCP** to a Workspace. No Task-studio, no Task preview-chat. | Google’s API may break. A real Tasks slice beats a fake twin of Agents. |
| D065 | 2026-09-25 | Helm: **kagent on / AX off** by default (appliance). Both on, or AX-only (no seed chat), are flags. AX+Substrate stay out of the default VM. | Don’t make the “no k8s” path pull Redis + Substrate. |
| D066 | 2026-09-25 | Skanyxx talks to runtimes through a **small port** (Agents vs Tasks). kagent adapter is complete; AX adapter is the v1 Tasks pack. | Hedge without two umbrellas. |
| D067 | 2026-09-25 | **Supersedes D065.** AX (+ Substrate + AX Redis) ships **in the Helm umbrella like kagent** and is **installed by default**. Values can still turn AX or kagent off. Jobs stay split (D063). | One chart, two engines. Not a separate AX install. |
| D068 | 2026-09-25 | **One Dragonfly** in the umbrella by default. AX uses it as its Redis (streams/queue). Memory may use the **same instance** later for cache/locks. **Cards stay in Postgres.** Values can still point at their Redis/Dragonfly. | One Redis-compatible box. Isolation was optional, not required. Supersedes “AX has its own Redis” and D043 “Dragonfly off until later.” |
| D078 | 2026-09-26 | **Refines D040 per D052** (memory default grants, as built). An agent with **no grants** searches `company` + the calling user's personal scope and may upsert **only that personal scope**. Explicit grants (`PUT /api/memory/grants/{agentId}`) **replace** the default; an empty grant list is rejected; a single grant with both rights false **revokes** the agent. | D040 ("search only on company") and D052 ("upsert defaults to personal") disagreed; the code follows D052. Company writes stay an explicit grant, so D040's storm guard holds. |
| D079 | 2026-09-26 | **People authenticate with ASP.NET Core Identity inside the Skanyxx API.** Local accounts, roles and sessions live in Postgres (`Skanyxx.Module.Identity`); browser = cookie, API/desktop = bearer + refresh (D059). **No bundled IdP** (no Keycloak etc. in the umbrella). Entra ID and other IdPs come later as **external-login mappers** onto these accounts. **Refines the mechanics of D026/D027**: invite still always works; IdP group → role mapping still admits only mapped users. | One fewer service to install and run (D018, D057 appliance). The org tree is Skanyxx's (D055), so accounts belong next to it; an IdP only maps into it. |
| D080 | 2026-09-26 | **Agents authenticate to the memory MCP with a per-agent secret issued by Skanyxx** (planned next slice). Until then `/mcp/memory` trusts `X-Agent-Id` / `X-User-Id` and **must be blocked at the ingress** (cluster network only). | A signed-in person and a running agent are different principals; agents need their own revocable credential, not a user session. The header interim is documented with its blast radius in the README. |
| D081 | 2026-09-26 | **Break-glass owner = the bootstrap token.** `Identity:BootstrapToken` (required outside Development, ≥ 32 chars) creates the first owner and later guards `POST /api/identity/unlock`, which clears the owner's lockout. Loopback-only bootstrap is Development-only. | Implements identity.md "Break-glass owner always works": a lockout attack on the only account must not end the install. Loopback is not a trust boundary in Kubernetes (sidecars, port-forward). |
| D082 | 2026-09-26 | **Refines D081 (as built).** Unlock alone loses to a sustained guesser, so the bootstrap token also works **at sign-in**: the owner presenting `X-Bootstrap-Token` (or the `/Login` token field) with the right password signs in even while locked out; the password is still checked and a wrong one still counts; other accounts and wrong tokens get the ordinary sign-in. Sign-in attempts on a busy account are refused at once (`pg_try_advisory_xact_lock`), not queued; the token holder waits its turn. The token must have no leading/trailing whitespace. Loopback bootstrap (Development) also requires a loopback `Host`. | Security round 2: queued guesses kept the owner locked out after unlock and pinned the identity DB pool; a DNS-rebound page could bootstrap a fresh dev instance with `AllowedHosts=*`. |
| D083 | 2026-09-26 | **D080 as built.** A supervisor issues an agent's memory secret with `POST /api/memory/agents/{agentId}/secret` (shown once; `GET` = status only, `DELETE` = revoke); issuing again rotates (one active secret per agent). `skx_mem_` + 256 CSPRNG bits, stored as SHA-256 only (unique index, looked up by hash). `/mcp/memory` requires `Authorization: Bearer <secret>` on every request (`initialize` and `tools/list` too) and answers anything else with `401`; the agent is the secret's owner and `X-Agent-Id` is no longer read. `X-User-Id` is read only on an authenticated agent call. kagent: one `RemoteMCPServer` per agent with the secret from a k8s Secret; rotation = issue → update Secret → change a label on the Agent (kagent reconciles on labels, re-renders the config and rolls the pod; a bare pod restart reloads the stale config). | kagent discovers tools with the RemoteMCPServer's own headers, so a shared server would need an extra discovery credential; one per agent keeps one secret per identity. X-User-Id is only as good as kagent's `auth.mode` (run it `secure`). Supersedes the ingress-block requirement of D080 (keeping `/mcp` off the public ingress stays advised). See README "Identity and security model". |
| D084 | 2026-09-26 | **Refines D083: acting for users is an owner-only, per-agent flag.** Each agent secret carries `acts_for_users` (default `false`), set by the issuing request's optional body `{"actsForUsers": true}`; only role `owner` may set it (`403` for a supervisor). Every issue sets it again, so a rotation that omits it turns it off. On `/mcp/memory`, `X-User-Id` is honoured only when the flag is on **and** the value is a Skanyxx user id (lowercase canonical GUID); otherwise the call has no user — no personal scope, personal upsert refused ("No user context"). Issue, rotation and revocation are logged at Warning (actor, agent, flag, client IP; never the secret). `/mcp` must not be on the public ingress and kagent → Skanyxx traffic must be mesh-mTLS or `https://` with the RemoteMCPServer `tls` block. | QA round 1 (SEC H1): with D083 any secret — and any supervisor, who can mint one — reached every user's personal memory by naming them, which the grant validator and REST deny. The flag keeps that power explicit, rare and the owner's decision. A non-GUID id (e.g. kagent's `admin@kagent.dev`) would silently pool many humans in one personal scope. Accepted risk: no expiry or last-used signal on secrets (open.md). |
| D085 | 2026-09-26 | **Refines D084: an acts-for-users secret is the owner's to rotate or revoke.** When the agent's current secret has `acts_for_users = true`, a supervisor's issue or revoke is `403` and the row is untouched; the check is the `WHERE` on the upsert's `ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE` and on the `DELETE`, so it is evaluated against the locked row. Refused issue/revoke attempts (`403`) are logged at Warning with actor, agent, reason (and requested flag). | QA round 2 (SEC L7): a supervisor rotating an owner-configured agent silently switched acting-for-users off (a downgrade/DoS by a lower role). SEC L6: the H1 escalation attempt left no trace. Audit IP is the direct peer (the ingress behind a proxy); the actor id attributes. |
| D086 | 2026-09-29 | **D026 as built (identity slice 2): invite by single-use link, owner-only people admin.** The owner creates an invite `{email, roles}` (`POST /api/identity/invites`; roles from `supervisor`/`builder`/`employee`, never `owner`) and gets the link once: `/Invite?token=skx_inv_<256 CSPRNG bits>`, stored as SHA-256 only, 7 days (`Identity:InviteDays`). An email that already has an account is refused (`409`); re-inviting an email revokes its open invite. Accepting (`/Invite` page or `POST /api/identity/invites/accept`) spends the invite with one conditional `UPDATE` under the email's account lock, creates the account with the invite's roles and signs it in (cookie or tokens); a rejected password rolls the spend back. Lookup/accept are anonymous, answer one generic `404` for unknown/used/revoked/expired, and count in the sign-in rate-limit window (the page on GET too). The owner lists people, replaces roles, disables/enables (disable = lockout until 9999 + sessions ended) and revokes invites; a role change or disable rotates the security stamp and drops refresh chains. The owner account keeps `owner` and cannot be disabled. Every action is logged at Warning with actor and target ids, never a token. The token rides in the query string / request body, never a path. | Contract for slice 2 (task 2026-09-29). Query/body instead of the planned `/invite/{token}` and `GET invites/{token}`: request logs and traces record paths (Serilog `RequestPath`, OTel `url.path`) and leave the query out or redact it. Remaining exposure: a reverse proxy's access log may record the query string — the link is single-use and short-lived. |
| D087 | 2026-09-29 | **Bearer access tokens are checked against the security stamp on every request** (resolves A3 of slice 2). The module wraps the bearer scheme's `OnMessageReceived`: it unprotects the token, and `SignInManager.ValidateSecurityStampAsync` failing makes the request `401`. Sign-out, a role change and disabling therefore end access tokens at once instead of after their ≤ 1 h lifetime; cookies still re-check every `Identity:SecurityStampValidationSeconds` (60 s). | ASP.NET Core's bearer handler checks only signature and expiry, so a demoted or disabled person kept up to an hour of the old roles. Cost: one primary-key read per bearer request, acceptable at this scale; a short-lived stamp cache would be the lever if it ever is not. |
| D088 | 2026-09-29 | **Refines D086/D087 (QA round 1 of identity slice 2).** (1) Invite links are built on `Identity:PublicBaseUrl`, validated at startup: absolute, no query; required outside Development and `https://` there unless its host is loopback (desktop installs, `http://localhost:5282`); Development without it uses the request. (2) Offboarding: when a role change removes `supervisor`, or a person is disabled, identity publishes `PrivilegesRevoked(userId, reason)` (MediatR notification in Core) after its commit, and the memory module deletes every `memory_agent_secrets` row with `created_by` = that user, logging each agent at Warning; owner-issued secrets are unaffected; nothing is restored on enable. (3) The owner's roles are fixed: `PUT people/{owner}/roles` is `403` (was: silently kept `owner` and signed the owner out). (4) Invite lookup/accept and `/Invite` get their own rate-limit window (`Skanyxx:InviteRateLimit`, 10/60 s), out of the sign-in window. (5) Passwords must not contain the email's local part (3+ chars, case-insensitive), for every password Identity sets. (6) Audit lines carry the client address; refused invite accepts/lookups and non-owners refused on owner-only routes are logged at Warning. **Accepted risks:** no breached/common-password list (length ≥ 12 + the local-part rule only); a disable racing a sign-in between its password check and cookie issue can leave that cookie working until the next stamp check (≤ `SecurityStampValidationSeconds`, 60 s) — bearer/refresh from that sign-in fail at once. | Security review round 1: M1 (link from `Request.Scheme`/`Host` is `http://` or an internal host behind a TLS-terminating proxy, putting a single-use account credential in cleartext), M2 (a demoted or disabled supervisor kept agent access through secrets they minted and copied), L1–L8 and code review CR1–CR6. Identity never touches the memory database: the notification keeps the modules independent. Loopback http is allowed because such a link never crosses a network. The cookie race costs one principal build under the account lock to close and is the same window as sign-out elsewhere. |
| D089 | 2026-09-29 | **Refines D087/D088 (QA round 2 of identity slice 2).** (1) **Cookies are checked against the security stamp on every request**, like bearer tokens: the module replaces Identity's interval stamp validator with one `SignInManager.ValidateSecurityStampAsync` per cookie request; `Identity:SecurityStampValidationSeconds` is removed (an old key is ignored). A role change, disable or sign-out refuses a cookie on its very next request, and a cookie from a sign-in that raced a disable carries the old stamp and is refused likewise — D088's accepted ≤ 60 s windows are gone. (2) `PrivilegesRevoked` is published whenever roles are saved **without** `supervisor` (unchanged roles too) and on every disable, with `CancellationToken.None`; a failing handler is logged at Error (actor, target) and rethrown (`500`), and saving the same change again is the retry. `GET /api/memory/agents/{id}/secret` shows `createdBy`. (3) `Identity:PublicBaseUrl` no longer stops startup when **unset**: outside Development the app logs a warning and invite creation answers `409` naming the setting, before any write. A **set** value must be written exactly (no whitespace, control characters or backslashes) and is otherwise validated as in D088; the link is built from the parsed URL. A loopback value while Skanyxx listens on a non-loopback address is warned about at startup, and the People page shows the link's host. (4) Owner-route refusal logs name the route template, only for someone holding none of the route's roles. | Security review round 2 N1: through the 60 s cookie window a demoted or disabled supervisor could mint an agent secret *after* the revocation ran, and it survived offboarding. Cost: one primary-key read per cookie request, as bearer already pays. N2 / code review M1: a cancelled or failed post-commit publish failed open and re-saving the roles re-drove nothing. Verifier round 2: requiring the key at startup crash-looped every upgraded desktop install (installers never edit an existing `appsettings.json`) over a feature a single-user install rarely uses; refusing at invite time keeps the invariant — no link is ever built from the request outside Development. |
| D090 | 2026-09-30 | **D055 as built (identity slice 3): the org tree and memory membership.** The identity module owns `identity_org_departments(slug, name)`, `identity_org_teams(slug, name, department)` and `identity_org_team_members(team, user)`; a department contains teams, a person is in 0..n teams, and department membership is **derived** from one's teams (never assigned). Slugs have the memory scope id shape (validator + a CHECK), are unique per kind, never change (rename = display name only; a team moves between departments) and are never reused (nothing is deleted in this slice). Only the owner manages it (`api/identity/org/...`, the **Org** page; `403` others, `401` anonymous; every change logged at Warning with actor, target and client address). Memory asks through Core's `IOrgMembership` (implemented by identity, in-process, one query per check, **no cache** — a change counts from the next request; nothing in tokens or claims) and never references the identity module (architecture test). **Rules:** humans read/search company, their personal scope, their teams and those teams' departments; **owner and supervisors read/search every team and department scope** (oversight) but **write — upsert, or lift into — a team or department only where they are members**, like anyone; lift into a team/department needs membership of the target, into company a supervisor (unchanged); a lift's source must be readable. A team/department slug with **no org object** has no members: only owner/supervisor read its cards (fail closed). **Agents** stay on grants only (D033, D078): an acts-for-users agent gets its user's personal scope, **never the user's teams**; team/department access is an explicit grant a supervisor sets. | Task 2026-09-30 (decisions D1–D5 there). Reading is oversight, writing is membership: a supervisor who could write every team would be one account away from rewriting any team's knowledge. Membership in the token would outlive a removal by the token's lifetime. Grants as the agent boundary keep one person's reach from leaking into a shared agent. |

Supervisors keep `company`/`personal` grants. (2) **Audit:** every grant change and grant refusal is logged at Warning (actor, agent, each scope with its flags, client address); every lift whose source is a team/department the caller is not a member of is logged at Warning (actor, source, target, key). A supervisor's lift into `company` stays allowed. (3) Every memory read check routes an agent through its grants — never through its user's membership. (4) A person refused a write is told who may write ("Only members of team:x may write there."); agents keep "No upsert grant". (5) Membership is asked once per request (cached on the request-scoped policy) and not at all for a supervisor's search. (6) Adding a team member takes the account lock disable/enable take and re-reads the account under it. (7) Memory declares `Dependencies => ["identity"]`; the Host refuses to start when an enabled module's dependency is not loaded, and memory's initialization fails without `IOrgMembership`. D002/D017: chat is Chat's alone. Task `2026-10-05_1700_skanyxx-first-hour`, review/code-reviewer-1.md. |
| D106 | 2026-10-05 | **Model step refinements (amends D099).** Keeping the stored key while the base URL changes would send that key to the new host, so a keyed provider with a different base URL and no new key is `409` ("paste the key again"). A switch between keyed providers saves twice — first without a key, so kagent deletes the Secret the spec no longer names, then with the new key — because kagent merges a new key into the existing Secret (`StringData`) and the old provider's live key would otherwise stay in the cluster. Ollama needs its host (no silent fallback to the agent pod's localhost); the owner may point it at any URL the agent pods reach (owner-trusted SSRF, documented). kagent 0.10.2's update has no precondition: saves are read-modify-write, last writer wins; a create that loses a race (`409`) is re-read and saved as an update once. | Code review 1 of slice 1 (M1, L1, L2, L7); kagent `handlers/modelconfig.go` (secret written by `StringData`, stale owned Secrets swept after the spec flip). |

No kagent call is ever retried — `message/send` and model writes are not idempotent — so the client is a plain named HttpClient without Polly. kagent failing (non-2xx, timeout, malformed or oddly typed JSON) is `502` with a fixed text for people; kagent's own text goes to the log (Warning; a foreign conversation's 404 is routine). The agents list failing is `502`, never "no such agent". A first turn that fails deletes the session it created. Skanyxx holds one git account (`Studio:Git:User`, default `skanyxx-bot` — not the org's name: Gitea users and orgs share a namespace) whose token is configuration, never logged or returned. **Setup creates the repo** automatically: identity publishes `OwnerBootstrapped` (Core notification) after the first owner commits, which only wakes the reconcile loop (never on the setup request, so setup never waits on or fails because of git); the loop's pass ensures org → repo → protection rule on `main` (`enable_push: false`, merge whitelist = that account, `block_admin_merge_override`), checks it, and records the repo (D119); it retries until git answers, but only until a repo was recorded; the loop idles until an owner exists, so a bare start creates nothing. **Propose** (builders and the owner): the form (D029: name — a letter first, at most 33 characters —, description, model from kagent's ModelConfigs, instructions, skills as digest-pinned OCI refs from allow-listed registries (D122), tools of allow-listed `Studio:McpServers`, search/upsert per `company`/`team:`/`department:` scope — never personal: a studio agent acts for nobody — and optional kagent TTL memory) → one commit on `studio/<name>-<utc>` authored by the builder → one PR whose body carries `Agent:` and `Proposed-by: <user id>`. The order is delete the old server → issue → create the server → mark it deployed; a pass that finds memory's live secret not the deployed one (a failure in between, another replica, a person's rotation) or no server issues again. kagent offers no read of a server's spec, so the mark lives in memory. | Issue-first left an agent on a revoked secret forever when the delete or create failed after it (the old skip test only asked "a secret and a server exist"). |

For the recorded repo every caller only **verifies**: the studio never re-creates a deleted rule on its own (anything pushed while it was gone would then be trusted), and a page view (overview, propose) never writes to git. The repo and its rule are created only by the reconciler's pass while no repo was ever recorded (setup); before that record, overview/propose/preview/merge answer "not set up yet". (Corrected in QA round 2, m1/m4/m6: a missing rule used to be re-created silently, and overview/propose could write it.) **Removal brake:** a pass removes no merged agent when main has no `agents/` while studio agents run, when more than `Studio:MaxRemovalsPerPass` (2) would go, or more than half of them (two or more). A lock connection that drops mid-pass also releases it unnoticed, so a second replica may start an overlapping pass (`KeepAlive=30` limits idle drops; D118's fingerprints make the overlap self-healing); the release itself never throws (any failure is a Warning and the connection is disposed, QA round 2 m2). Session locks need a session-mode connection: a transaction-mode pooler (pgbouncer) between Skanyxx and memory's Postgres would break them. The periodic pass skips when another holds it; a merge, preview, factory call or suspend waits up to `Studio:LockWaitSeconds` (20), then a merge answers `202` (merged, applied by the next pass) and the others `503`. The loop catches everything a pass throws except shutdown: transient failures at Warning, the rest at Error, and it runs again on the next tick (or when poked, e.g. by setup). | The `SemaphoreSlim` held within one process only; identity already plans for replicas. | D123 | 2026-10-06 | **Studio robustness from QA round 1 (M1 and minors).** Untrusted YAML is scanned before anything is built from it: no anchors or aliases, nesting ≤ 16, ≤ 5,000 nodes, one document (an alias bomb took ~900 MB from 256 bytes, deep nesting overflowed the stack). git answers are read with a limit — agent files 64 KB (refused before the body is read in full), JSON 8 MB. Description and instructions refuse control characters and Unicode format characters (bidi overrides, zero-width), as display names do (QA round 2 I4). | D146 | 2026-10-06 | **The Host exits 1 on a startup failure (QA round 1, H4; fixes D135's finding).** Program.cs's top level is now only `try { await Program.RunAsync(args); return 0; } catch (not HostAbortedException) { Log.Fatal(...); return 1; } finally { Log.CloseAndFlush(); }`; the former body is `Program.RunAsync`. No rethrow, so no `abort()` that a container's PID 1 ignores. | D152 | 2026-10-07 | **The durable identity audit (task D1).** `identity_audit(Id bigint identity, AtUtc, Action, ActorId, TargetId, RemoteIp, Details jsonb)`, no FKs (a row outlives what it names), indexes on time, actor and target. **Append-only:** a trigger refuses every `UPDATE`; only the retention job deletes. Every Warning audit line in identity also writes a row (`Audit/AuditActions.cs`: invites created/revoked/accepted/refused, lookups refused, roles, disable/enable, Microsoft login removed, bootstrap-token sign-ins, managed accounts' refused passwords and refreshes, the org changes, Entra settings saves and turn-on, accounts created/re-mapped/linked/refused by Entra, refused links and callbacks, owner-route refusals) plus the new flows (password resets, the Graph re-check, retention purges). **Same transaction:** a change's row is one `INSERT` on the handler's own connection inside its transaction, written before the session revocation, so a failure anywhere after it rolls both back (the handlers that had no transaction — invite revoke, org writes — now have one); a refusal changes nothing, so its row is written on its own — after an explicit rollback when the refusal happens inside a transaction. `Details` never holds a token, password or secret (the Entra secret is "replaced"/"kept"); it does hold an invited email address (PII the invites table already holds; retention bounds it). Owner-only `GET /api/identity/audit?before&limit(1–200, 50)&action&userId` (newest first, keyset by id; `userId` matches actor or target) and the **Audit** page (owner nav; filters, "Older", ids shown as emails). The Warning lines stay. | open.md: invite/role/disable/org/Entra changes were log lines only. Writing the row with the change is what makes "a failed action leaves no row" hold without an outbox. A trigger keeps a compromised application role from quietly editing history (it can still delete — the retention job needs that). |

With a second `PrivilegesRevoked` handler (D154), an unreachable AX would otherwise keep memory from revoking a disabled person's agent secrets — whichever handler happened to run first decided. |
| D154 | 2026-10-07 | **A disable stops the person's sandbox tasks (task D4; open.md offboarding).** `PrivilegesRevoked` gains `AccountDisabled`, read from the account when it is published (so a retried revocation of a disabled account carries it, and one published by an enable does not). The sandboxes module handles it: for a disabled account, every **active** task (Pending, Running, Suspended) whose recorded owner is that person is deleted through AX (the module's "stop"), each under its task lock and re-read there; Warning per task. **Supervisor loss stops nothing** (ownership is decided per request, and a demoted person's own tasks stay theirs); **workspaces stay** (storage, not running code). Listing pages to an empty page and refuses past 10,000 tasks. AX unreachable or a task that will not stop: the rest are still tried, then the handler throws — identity answers `500` with the change saved and the revocation owed, and disabling again retries (every disable publishes). Sandboxes off (`Sandboxes:Enabled` false): nothing, and the AX client is never built. Host tests that disable people run with sandboxes off; one test shows the `500`. Also on an Entra re-check refusal (D161); the `500` says what did not happen (D162); NotFound/any-exception handling D163. | D4 of the task. A disabled person must not keep code running in their name. Failing loud keeps D089's contract: a revocation that did not happen is never reported as done. |
| D155 | 2026-10-07 | **Retention (task D1; open.md "invite rows are never purged").** `Identity:AuditRetentionDays` (365, 1–3650). A job on every replica — one minute after start, then every 6 h — deletes audit rows older than that, invites that **stopped being pending** before it (`COALESCE(AcceptedUtc, RevokedUtc, ExpiresUtc) < cutoff`; a pending invite always expires in the future), and password-reset links a day after they expired; when it removed audit rows or invites it writes an `audit.retention_purged` row with the counts. Plain `DELETE`s: safe on several replicas at once (in batches since D169). `identity_invites.AcceptedUserId` now has its FK (`ON DELETE SET NULL`; dangling values nulled first). | Bounds how long PII in audit details and invites lives. The purge's own row keeps the trail honest about its gaps. |
| D156 | 2026-10-07 | **Password reset by email (task D2).** "Forgot your password?" on `/Login` (shown only with SMTP and a buildable link) → `/ForgotPassword`, or `POST /api/identity/password/forgot {email}` → **`202` with the same body for every email**. Nothing is looked up before answering: the request goes on an in-process bounded queue (256, a full queue drops it) and a background worker, under the account lock, issues a link only to an account that may reset (D157) and asked no more than once per **2 minutes**; refusals (unknown email, owner, disabled, managed, too soon) send nothing and are audited. The link: `<PublicBaseUrl>/ResetPassword?token=skx_rst_<256 CSPRNG bits>`, stored as SHA-256 only, **single use**, `Identity:PasswordResetMinutes` (60, 5–1440), one open link per account (a new one revokes the older). Completing it (`/ResetPassword` page, or `POST /api/identity/password/lookup {token}` and `POST /api/identity/password/reset {token, password}` → `204`) spends it with a conditional `UPDATE` under the account lock, re-checks the account, replaces the password (the usual rules), clears the lockout and failed count, revokes other open links and ends every session (stamp + refresh chains), audited in that transaction; a rejected password rolls all of it back and the link still works; unknown/used/revoked/expired are one `404`. No session is started: the person signs in. Forgot (API and page) counts in the sign-in window; lookup, reset and the reset page (GET too) in the one-time-link window (`Skanyxx:InviteRateLimit`). The page answers `no-store` + `no-referrer`. SMTP off: forgot is `404` ("…ask the owner for a reset link"), a configuration answer that says nothing about accounts. | NIST/OWASP shape: no enumeration (body and timing), a long random hashed single-use short-lived token, sessions ended on reset. The queue is what makes the timing constant; a restart loses what waited, which looks the same to the asker. |
| D157 | 2026-10-07 | **Who may reset, and the owner's reset link (task D2).** Refused: **the owner** (break-glass stays the bootstrap token, unchanged; `403`), **a disabled account** (`409`, enable it first), **an Entra-managed account while Microsoft sign-in is on** (`409` "This account signs in with Microsoft.", D8). While it is off, a managed account may reset — which gives an account Entra created its first password (open.md "no password reset" for them). The owner issues a link from **People** ("Password reset link") or `POST /api/identity/people/{id}/password-reset` → `201 {link, expiresAt, emailed}`: emailed to the person when SMTP is on (`link: null`), otherwise — or when sending fails — shown once to the owner (`no-store`), to pass on. Issuing changes nothing else (the old password works until the link is used). The account is checked again when the link is used, so a link issued before a disable stops working. | D2 of the task. The owner already controls every account but must not be able to sign in as one; with SMTP the owner never holds the person's credential. |
| D158 | 2026-10-07 | **Entra group re-check between sign-ins (task D3; open.md "groups are read only at a Microsoft sign-in").** A job every `Identity:EntraRecheckMinutes` (60, 5–1440) while Microsoft sign-in can be used (on, secret readable, groups mapped): one replica at a time (`pg_try_advisory_xact_lock(0x49444E06)`; since D164 held only to claim the sweep, once per interval across replicas), every Entra-managed account except the owner, disabled accounts and keys of another tenant (counted in one Warning) is asked about through Graph `checkMemberGroups` (app-only, the mapped ids only, ≤ 20 per call; one token per sweep, reused while valid) and the answer applied under the account lock: still mapped → re-mapped exactly as a sign-in (a role change ends the sessions; teams change on the next request); **no mapped group left — or the user gone from the tenant (Graph `404`) — is a refused sign-in (D2):** roles and teams removed, every session ended, the account kept. Losing `supervisor` publishes `PrivilegesRevoked` (owed-mark rules of D095/D096; a failure is retried by the next re-check, sign-in or People save). Nothing is written when nothing changes (except a first refusal, D161). Graph unreachable: nothing changes, one Warning, the sweep stops until the next tick. Audited (`entra.recheck_remapped`/`_refused`). Guest status is not re-checked (it needs a token; accounts are only created or linked for members). | Bounds how long a person removed in Entra keeps their Skanyxx roles to the interval, instead of their next Microsoft sign-in or `Identity:SessionDays`. Per-request checks from the stamp validator (open.md's other option) would put Graph on every request; not built. |
| D159 | 2026-10-07 | **Section 6 identity: shapes, deviations and what is left.** `GET /api/identity/status` gains `passwordResetByEmail`. `GraphMembership` keeps its token for the instance's life while valid (a sign-in resolves one instance; the sweep one). Advisory key `0x49444E06` is listed with the others. Deviations from the task's defaults: the forgot form counts in the **sign-in** window and the reset link in the **one-time-link** window (the invite window, renamed in docs), so crawlers opening emailed links cannot spend sign-ins; the owner's link is **not returned when emailed** (D157), and the same for invites (D151). **Not built / accepted:** an owner who forgot their password still has no in-app path (break-glass signs in with the password; a database-level reset is the runbook); no breached-password check (D088); the reset queue is in-process (a restart loses waiting requests); per-request Graph checks; email delivery is not retried (the owner flows fall back to the link; a lost self-service email is asked again). | Recorded so the next slice starts from what exists. |
| D160 | 2026-10-07 | **Notification handlers run in a declared order, and a cancelled publish is cancellation (QA round 1: verifier G1, CR L4).** `NotificationOrderAttribute` (Core) orders handlers under `AllHandlersPublisher` (lower first, 0 when absent, registration order between equals): memory's `RevokeSecretsOnPrivilegesRevoked` is `Revocation` (-100), the sandbox stop `External` (100). The publisher checks the token before each handler and lets an `OperationCanceledException` the token caused go out as itself (no aggregate) — unless a handler already failed, when the failures and the cancellation go out together as an `AggregateException`, so a cancellation never hides a failure (amended in QA round 2). Shown on the real host: AX unreachable, a disable answers `500` and the supervisor's agent secret is already refused on `/mcp/memory`; the composed order is memory, then sandboxes. | D153 ran handlers in registration order, so a slow AX (15 s per call plus paging) could delay the secret revocation by that long. Every current publisher passes `CancellationToken.None`; the cancellation rule keeps a future one from turning a client abort into a 500. |
| D161 | 2026-10-07 | **An Entra re-check refusal removes access, once (QA round 1, orchestrator decision; CR L2/L3, supersedes verifier G5).** When the Graph re-check refuses an account (no mapped group left, or the user gone from the tenant), the first refusal — marked by an `identity_user_tokens` row (`entra` / `recheck_refused`, never in a principal) — ends every session **even when no role or team changed**, audits it, and owes a revocation published with the new `PrivilegesRevoked.AccessRemoved` flag; the sandboxes handler stops the person's active tasks for `AccountDisabled` **or** `AccessRemoved`. `AccessRemoved` is read at publish (disabled, or marked), so a failed stop is retried by the next sweep with the same meaning (the failure is logged at Error and the sweep goes on). Later sweeps of a refused account change nothing. The mark is cleared when the mapping grants access again (re-check or Microsoft sign-in) and when the owner removes the Microsoft login. A **refused Microsoft sign-in** does not set it: the next re-check does (within `EntraRecheckMinutes`), so an unmapped tenant user cannot make Skanyxx list AX tasks by signing in. A disable now publishes `AccessRemoved: true` too. **Amended in QA round 2 (L2):** a settings save that leaves Microsoft sign-in unusable (off, or no group mapped) or changes the tenant deletes every mark in its transaction (audited as `refusalsCleared`), and `PrivilegeRevocation` honours a mark only while the cached settings can sign in — with sign-in off the person uses their password again (D8), so a later role save must not stop their tasks. | A person removed from Entra must not keep code running in their name (D154's reason), nor a session that a role-less mapping never rotated. The mark is what makes "once" hold without a new table. |
| D162 | 2026-10-07 | **A saved change whose revocation failed says so (QA round 1, verifier G2).** Core `RevocationFailedException` carries an owner-facing message: the sandbox handler throws it ("Its sandbox tasks could not be stopped (AX unavailable)", or "N of its M sandbox tasks could not be stopped …"); `PrivilegeRevocation` rethrows any handler failure as one (handlers' own words, else "Not everything the person issued could be revoked."); a disable/enable wraps it as "Account disabled. … — disable again to retry.", and a roles save (a disabled or refused account, QA round 2 L3) as "Roles saved. … — save again to retry.". The API answers `500` problem details with that `detail` (Core `RevocationFailedProblem`, API paths only); the People page catches it and shows the line on the page with status `500`. Never a secret or AX's own error text. | The owner saw a generic error page for a change that was in fact saved. |
| D163 | 2026-10-07 | **Sandbox stop: a vanished task is stopped, and every failure is per task (QA round 1, CR L5).** `NotFound` on the delete (another replica or the person deleted it after the in-lock read) counts as stopped. Any exception from one task (not only `RpcException`/`InvalidOperationException`) is counted and the rest are still tried, except the caller's own cancellation; a listing failure is "AX unavailable". Not mutation-tested: a non-RPC failure has no seam in the gRPC fake. | KeyedLock is per process, so the race is real with replicas; a single unexpected exception type used to skip the remaining tasks. |
| D164 | 2026-10-07 | **One Entra sweep per interval across replicas, and Graph misconfiguration is an Error (QA round 1, CR L6).** `identity_job_runs(Name, LastRunUtc)`. Each replica looks a minute after start and then every `min(5 min, interval)`; under `pg_try_advisory_xact_lock(0x49444E06)` one conditional upsert records the database's `now()` only when the last start is an interval old by `now()` (QA round 2: the replica's clock no longer matters), and commits — the lock is held for that claim, not for the sweep, so no transaction idles through Graph calls and a restart never postpones an overdue sweep by a whole interval. Graph `403` (no `GroupMember.Read.All` admin consent) or `400` (a mapped group id Graph rejects) stops the sweep with one Error naming the cause; other failures stay a Warning. Not changed: the sweep does not re-read the Entra switch per account (turning sign-in off mid-sweep still applies the rest). | `PeriodicTimer` per replica meant N sweeps per interval and none at all with restarts more frequent than the interval; a lost consent made the feature silently do nothing. |
| D165 | 2026-10-07 | **Reset links do not survive a disable or a refused use (QA round 1, CR M1).** A disable revokes the account's open reset links in its transaction (the audit row counts them). A link used while its account is refused (owner, disabled, Entra-managed while on) is revoked in that transaction instead of rolled back, so enabling the account later cannot revive it. Completing a reset already revoked the other links; there is no other password-change path (invite accept creates the first password, bootstrap is the owner's). Amends D157's "a link issued before a disable stops working" — now also after the enable. | An owner disabling an account over a compromised mailbox and enabling it within the hour handed the attacker's link back. |
| D166 | 2026-10-07 | **A managed account's refused password writes the same row either way (QA round 1, CR M2).** `signin.managed_password_refused` (target, no actor, no details) is written for a right and a wrong password alike, before the failure count, with the same Warning log line either way (QA round 2 L1); only a Debug line says the password was right. | The row used to be written only for the right password: one database round trip of difference, a timing oracle on a password that works again when Microsoft sign-in is turned off. Bounded by the lockout (a locked account takes the hash-only branch). |
| D167 | 2026-10-07 | **Anonymous-origin audit rows are sampled (QA round 1, verifier G4, CR L7).** `IdentityAudit.WriteSampledAsync` + `AuditSampler` (singleton, in memory): refusals any caller can cause — forgot requests that send nothing (per reason), invalid reset/invite lookups, unknown reset/invite tokens at use, failed Microsoft callbacks, owner-route refusals (per route and caller, QA round 2 L4: callers are signed in, so a second person refused on a route gets their own row; bounded by people × routes) — write at most one row per action and kind per minute per replica, with `skippedBefore` (how many were left out since the previous row). Every Warning line stays. `OwnerRouteRefusals` and the callback no longer pass `RequestAborted` (an abort must not throw out of authorization). Refusals naming a found invite or account at use (no longer pending, rejected password) keep a row each. | A spread-IP flood could grow `identity_audit` by one row per request for 365 days and bury real events; per-IP rate limits do not bound it. Per replica rather than global: N replicas write at most N rows a minute per kind, which is the bound that matters. |
| D168 | 2026-10-07 | **Email sends are bounded, logged once, and a shown link is audited (QA round 1, verifier G3, CR L1/L10).** `SmtpEmailSender` caps the whole send at `2 × TimeoutSeconds` (MailKit's timeout is per operation) and follows the caller's token: the owner's request for invites and owner reset links, the worker's stop for self-service. It no longer logs; `LinkMail` writes the one Warning. When SMTP is on but the send failed, a second row records that the owner was shown the link: `invite.link_shown` / `password.reset_link_shown`. Owner-issued sends stay inline (not queued) so "shown once when not emailed" keeps working. | The People POST could hang for about a minute on an unreachable server; one failure was two Warnings; the audit said "email" for a link the owner ended up holding. |
| D169 | 2026-10-07 | **Audit storage hardening and the rest of QA round 1 (CR L8/L9/L11, verifier G5).** Migration `AuditHardeningAndJobRuns`: a `BEFORE TRUNCATE … FOR EACH STATEMENT` trigger on `identity_audit` (same function as the UPDATE one; there is one database role, so a REVOKE would not bind the app — the owner can still disable triggers, as before), an `(Action, Id)` index for the Audit page's action filter, and `identity_job_runs` (D164). The Audit page's address column is "Client (direct peer)" with a tooltip (the proxy behind one). Retention deletes in batches of 5,000 until a batch is short; each batch commits alone and the `audit.retention_purged` row is written after them (amends D155's single transaction). L11 (the forgot queue can be starved) stays accepted as in D159. Verifier G5 is superseded by D161. README: reset and invite tokens in the query string appear in reverse-proxy access logs unless the log format drops the query. | A row trigger does not fire on TRUNCATE; the first purge after an upgrade was one unbounded DELETE. |

Its only user was the deleted root monolith (D171). A v8 replacement on `Microsoft.Extensions.Http.Resilience` was written first and dropped in QA round 1: no caller, and it pinned Polly.Core 8.4.2 below what Polly.RateLimiting 8.6.5 had pulled. No HTTP client in the solution retries: the kagent client stays a plain named client, infinite `HttpClient.Timeout`, per-call deadlines, no handler (D107); Gitea, Jira, Graph, Tickets-stage and AX are unchanged. | D176 | 2026-10-07 | **`first-hour.sh up` owns `localhost:8083` or refuses.** A supervisor loop is recognised only by its WHOLE command line (`ps -ww`, anchored to what `up` runs: `[<path>/]bash -c while true; do "$@" … port-forward kubectl --context <ctx> -n kagent port-forward svc/kagent-controller 8083:8083`), so a reused pid or a process that merely mentions that text (a `ps \| grep`) is never signalled. `up` finds EVERY such loop (`pgrep -f`, then that match; no pid file — macOS purges idle `$TMPDIR` files): one for `$KUBE_CONTEXT` is kept, the rest — another context, or a duplicate — are paused, then stopped with all their descendants (collected while paused; a loop's own just-forked child, which carries the same command line, is skipped), even when they hold no port at the moment (they would win `:8083` at our next kubectl restart). Then EVERY process listening on `:8083` (`lsof`, IPv4 and IPv6 — `localhost` tries `::1` first) must run under the kept loop (its parent, or up to three levels up for a kubectl wrapper); anything else → exit 1 naming the pid (after up to 10 s only when a loop was just stopped). `up` also exits 1 unless `/health` answers with only its own forward listening. `status` lists the loops (context each) and names who holds `:8083`. `lsof` and `pgrep` join the script's needs. A loop the script did not start is not recognised — if it holds `:8083`, `up` refuses; if it is idle, it is not found. Checked on OrbStack (task `2026-10-07_1845_skanyxx-orbstack-move`, evidence/d176-scenarios.md). | Moving to OrbStack, a 9-day-old hand-made port-forward loop to the kind cluster still held `:8083`: `up` saw a healthy `/health`, started nothing, and `/Model` plus Chat went to kind's kagent (whose seed had an old memory secret → 401) while every check passed. Reviews: a pid-file check misses a foreign listener while the supervisor lives and forgets idle loops; one listener checked misses a foreign `[::1]:8083`; an unanchored match could stop a process that only mentions the loop. |

| D178 | 2026-10-08 | **Sandbox network isolation is applied to the Substrate WorkerPool's namespace.** Substrate runs each sandbox inside a WorkerPool pod (gVisor), so `deploy/sandboxes` egress policies target that namespace; there is no separate sandbox-pod namespace. Verified on kind + gVisor (arm64): `sandbox-egress-deny` blocks the internet, `ax-server`, Substrate's API and the developer's Host from inside a sandbox, while Substrate keeps scheduling, running and stopping tasks. Not verified on GKE or micro-VM sandboxes; re-test there before `Sandboxes:NetworkIsolationConfirmed=true`. | Real-AX spike 2026-10-08 (task `2026-10-08_0830_skanyxx-ax-real-spike`, evidence/net-*.txt). | D179 | 2026-10-09 | **Sandboxes API shape: the resource is the sandbox (implements D071).** Routes are `GET /api/sandboxes?limit=&offset=` (list, exactly `/api/sandboxes`), `GET`/`PUT /api/sandboxes/{name}` (read / run), `POST /api/sandboxes/{name}/stop|suspend|resume`, `GET /api/sandboxes/{name}/watch` (SSE); workspaces and models keep `/api/sandboxes/workspaces[/{name}]` and `/api/sandboxes/models`. `workspaces` and `models` are **reserved sandbox names**: a run with either is `400` with a message saying so, because the literal routes win over `{name}` and such a sandbox could never be read back. Every module type, folder, test and page string uses the sandbox noun (`Sandbox`, `SandboxEvent`, `RunSandboxCommand`, `StopSandboxesOnPrivilegesRevoked`, …); only AX's generated proto types, `System.Threading.Tasks`, `Sandboxes:MaxActiveTasks*` config keys and comments naming AX's own RPCs keep "Task", and an architecture test fails on any other module type whose name contains it. JSON shapes are unchanged (a watch frame still carries the sandbox as `task`); owner-facing texts now say "sandbox" (e.g. "Its sandboxes could not be stopped (AX unavailable)", replacing D162's "sandbox tasks" wording; Identity's audit text follows: "sessions ended, sandboxes stopped"). **This breaks the experimental `/api/sandboxes/tasks/*` routes** (now `404`), with no alias. | D071 was locked but the code still said Task (~47 types, `/tasks` routes). The break is acceptable: the module is off by default and experimental, and the only consumer was the `/Sandboxes` page (5fa2794), changed in the same commit; no chart, script or other module calls the routes. A flat `/api/sandboxes/{name}` reads as the resource it is, and an alias would keep the forbidden noun alive. |
| D180 | 2026-10-09 | **Card bodies larger than the card go to an S3 bucket (todo §2 box 3; D014/D053 as built).** The memory module gets `Memory:ObjectStore` (`Endpoint`, `Bucket` default `skanyxx-memory`, `AccessKey`, `SecretKey`, `Region` default `us-east-1`, `ThresholdBytes` default 4096 (1–20 000, the Postgres column), `MaxBodyBytes` default 1 MiB, at most 8 MiB). **Off unless `Endpoint` is set:** every body stays in Postgres, capped at 20 000 characters, exactly as before; set but incomplete (no keys, a bad bucket name, a non-http(s) endpoint, max ≤ threshold) stops startup. On: a body over the threshold (UTF-8 bytes) is written to the bucket, Postgres keeps an excerpt, `source` the pointer (D181); the body cap becomes `MaxBodyBytes`, and only the upsert route's request limit grows to fit it (256 KB + 6 × max, JSON may escape every byte — about 48 MiB at the 8 MiB ceiling, buffered per request, which is why the ceiling is 8 MiB and not more), every other memory route keeps 256 KB. Client: **AWSSDK.S3 4.0** (path-style, checksums only when required, 15 s timeout and 2 retries so a black-holed store fails in about a minute; one version for Host and module in `src/AwsSdk.props`) — it speaks to MinIO and to real S3/BYO alike, which a MinIO-only client does not promise; like MailKit (D150) the Host references it and `PluginLoadContext` shares `AWSSDK*`. A store that does not answer is `502` and nothing is written — a refused connection, an S3 service error (wrong keys, AccessDenied, 5xx, SlowDown: in AWSSDK v4 these are not `AmazonClientException`s) or an SDK timeout alike. A body with an unpaired surrogate is refused (400) on both paths: it has no UTF-8 form (Postgres would fail the write, the store would get U+FFFD). Text bodies only; attaching files is not built. | The bank stays small and searchable (cards in Postgres, D042) while a long write-up still has a home a person can open. Off by default keeps every existing install and the local dev stack unchanged. |
| D181 | 2026-10-09 | **Pointer and object layout: content-addressed.** Object key `cards/sha256/<hex>` (SHA-256 of the body's UTF-8 bytes, `text/plain; charset=utf-8`); the card's `source` is `s3://<bucket>/cards/sha256/<hex>` and its `body` the first 500 characters (`CardLimits.BodyExcerpt`, never splitting a surrogate pair). The full text is never in Postgres, so FTS is unchanged (it never indexed bodies). Reading resolves only pointers into the **configured** bucket, through a card (D183): the key is not a capability. A body at or under the threshold stays inline; a new inline body on a stored card clears the pointer. | `source` is D014's blob layer, so a body moved out of the card lands there, as the todo box said. Content addressing makes lift free (D182), makes a retried PUT idempotent, and needs no rename handling (the key does not contain scope or card key). |
| D182 | 2026-10-09 | **`source` is memory's while it holds a pointer; lift shares it; objects are never deleted.** (1) Only memory writes the pointer shape: a caller's `source` matching `s3://…/cards/sha256/<64 hex>` is refused (400) unless it is the card's current pointer — with the store off too (the one store-off change; nobody types that shape), so a saved pointer cannot be pinned onto another card to keep reading a body after losing access. (2) A large body never drops a link silently: if the card has, or the write sends, a non-pointer `source`, the write is refused — put the link in the body and send `source: ""` to drop it. (3) On a stored card, a write with no body or with a body equal to the excerpt is "body unchanged" (so an agent's MCP upsert, which never carries body/source, and a REST client writing back what it read both keep the stored body); sending a different `source` without a new body is refused (it would detach the pointer); and a body that is NOT the excerpt but is short, sent with the current pointer, is refused (400, `body`): that is a client editing the excerpt it read, and taking it would silently replace the stored body — send the edited full text, or `source: ""` to replace it with a short body on purpose. The REST card carries `bodyStored` so a client can tell the body is an excerpt. (4) **Lift is a shared pointer, not a copy:** the lifted row copies `body` (the excerpt) and `source`, one object serves both; content addressing makes that safe. (5) Rename changes nothing in the store; a new version with a new large body writes a new object. (6) No card delete exists, and the app never deletes objects: an overwritten body's object stays (orphan, a cheap history). A sweep (bucket keys minus pointers still in `memory_cards`) is later work, and must account for lifted copies. | Overloading `source` needs rules so neither a forged pointer nor a silent overwrite is possible. Copy-on-lift would double storage for the common company lift and need a second key scheme; deletion with shared pointers needs reference counting, which a sweep does better than every write path. |
| D183 | 2026-10-09 | **Who reads a stored body: whoever may open the card; agents never.** `GET /api/memory/cards/{scope}/{key}/body` (FastEndpoints, people only like the rest of `/api/memory`) sends `GetCardBodyQuery`, whose handler opens the card through `GetCardQuery` — the scope's read rule, then D103 for company drafts — so the body follows exactly the card's `AccessPolicy` (403 another person's personal or team card, 404 a draft to an employee, 401 anonymous). It streams the object (or the inline Postgres body; 404 when the card has none) as `text/plain; charset=utf-8` with `nosniff`, `Content-Security-Policy: sandbox; default-src 'none'` and `Cache-Control: private, no-store`, so a body that is HTML never runs on Skanyxx's origin. A pointer while the store is off is `502`, never the excerpt passed off as the body. The Library shows the excerpt and an "Open the full body" link to that route (a navigation sends no `Origin`, so the origin guard passes; the cookie authenticates); `CardDto.BodyStored` tells the page (and REST clients). **MCP and search are unchanged and return neither body, excerpt nor pointer** (`CardHit` has no such fields; memory-card.md's prompt columns stay locked) — tested on MCP `memory_search` and REST search with a stored body. | D014: prompts only see the card. One rule for card and body means no second access path to keep in sync; the excerpt is still a body, so it obeys the same rule. |
| D184 | 2026-10-09 | **Chart: the bundled MinIO feeds Skanyxx; the app creates the bucket.** With `minio.enabled` the Skanyxx Deployment gets `Memory__ObjectStore__Endpoint=http://<release>-minio.<ns>.svc:9000`, `__Bucket` = `minio.bucket` (default `skanyxx-memory`), `__AccessKey`/`__SecretKey` from the MinIO Secret (`minio.existingSecret` when set — then hashed into `checksum/byo-secrets` like the other BYO Secrets), `__ThresholdBytes`/`__MaxBodyBytes` from `skanyxx.memoryBodies`. Without MinIO, `external.objectStore.endpoint` + `.bucket` + `.existingSecret` (keys `access-key`, `secret-key`) + optional `.region` do the same (the chart refuses an endpoint without bucket or Secret); with neither, nothing is set (store off). **No init job:** the first stored body that meets `NoSuchBucket` creates the bucket and retries (a race with another replica is tolerated); nothing at startup depends on MinIO being up, and no health check was added (MinIO down must not pull Skanyxx out of the Service). Outside us-east-1 the create passes the region as its location constraint. BYO keys that may not create buckets need the bucket created beforehand. The schema requires an `http(s)://` endpoint, and an external endpoint with `minio.enabled=true` is refused (it would be ignored). The ConfigMap `<release>-stores` now also names the bundled bucket. `values.schema.json`: bucket names, `memoryBodies` ranges, `external.objectStore.region`; `validate.sh` asserts the bundled and BYO wiring, the store-off render, and 5 new refusals (23 total). The existing NetworkPolicy already admits Skanyxx → MinIO. Not shown on a live cluster in this slice (render + kubeconform only; the app side is tested against the same MinIO image). | The app already holds the credentials; an init job would need an `mc` image, another Secret consumer and ordering against MinIO's readiness for no gain. |

Only errors: `no_auth` from the Dragonfly chart's own password-less probe, the expected `BUSYGROUP`, and one `syntax_error` per new connection = go-redis's `CLIENT MAINT_NOTIFICATIONS` probe, which stock Redis 7.4 rejects too and go-redis then disables. Not tested: Dragonfly with snapshots/persistence, more than one proactor thread, load. | D074 required a smoke test before AX may use the shared Dragonfly. Task `2026-10-09_1130_skanyxx-fix-partials`, W2/evidence (dfly-*.txt, watch-*.txt, restore-*.txt). |

Images are values with no default (AX publishes none; build with `ko` from v0.3.1); ax-server and ax-controller need a `digest` (`sha256:` only; a tag alone is refused, D075). | D188 | 2026-10-11 | **The chart refuses `ax.networkIsolationConfirmed=true` unless it renders the sandbox fence itself.** `ax.sandboxes.namespace` (the Substrate WorkerPool's namespace, D178) renders `deploy/sandboxes`' policies there: `<release>-sandbox-egress-deny` (DNS only, to `ax.sandboxes.dnsPeer`: default `kube-system` pods `k8s-app=kube-dns`, a `key=value` list so an override replaces it) and, with `ax.sandboxes.internet`, `<release>-sandbox-egress-internet` (0.0.0.0/0 and ::/0 minus `networkPolicy.clusterCidrs`, D149). `networkIsolationConfirmed` needs that namespace and `networkPolicy.enabled`; the release namespace is refused as the WorkerPool namespace (every policy there trusts it). | D189 | 2026-10-11 | **AX's Redis when the bundled Dragonfly is off:** `external.redis` (address + `existingSecret`, key `password`) if set, else **AX's own Redis** (google/ax `deploy/redis.yaml`, `ax.redis.image`, default `redis:7.4-alpine` pinned by digest) as `<release>-ax-redis`: a NetworkPolicy admits only the chart's `ax-server` and `ax-controller`, and unlike upstream it has a password (generated into the kept Secret `<release>-ax-redis`, the same on upgrade, `--requirepass` and `REDIS_PASSWORD` on both AX pods) so a CNI that does not enforce policies leaves it closed; non-root 999, read-only root fs with an emptyDir `/data`, `--save "" --appendonly no --maxmemory <80% of limits.memory> --maxmemory-policy noeviction` (without `maxmemory`, noeviction never triggers and Redis grows into the OOM kill). `external.redis.address` must be `host:port`.
