| D190 | 2026-10-11 | **D076/D077 as built: the sandbox listener.** New `Memory:SandboxMcpPort` (unset = no listener, no sandbox token accepted anywhere): a Kestrel port that serves `/mcp/memory` and nothing else — the existing port matcher (D138) admits the MCP route only on `McpPort` + `SandboxMcpPort` (when `McpPort` is set; unset, MCP is on every port as before), and `McpPortOnlyFilter` answers 404 for every other path on either port (UI, API, `/health`, pages, static files). Two authentication schemes guard the one MCP route, each bound to the connection's **local port** (never the Host header): the agent-secret scheme (D083) answers `NoResult` and stays silent on the sandbox port, the new sandbox-token scheme (`MemorySandboxToken`) does so everywhere else, so an agent secret is worth nothing on the sandbox port and a sandbox token nothing off it, and only the port's own scheme writes the 401. On the sandbox port the caller is **only** the token: `X-Agent-Id` and `X-User-Id` are never read (no code path reads a header for a sandbox principal). Token: `skx_sbx_<base64url(JSON {v, sbx, agt, own, exp, jti})>.<base64url(HMAC-SHA256(Memory:SandboxTokenKey, all before the dot))>`; signature checked in constant time before any claim is parsed; ≤ 1 KB. `Memory:SandboxTokenKey` (UTF-8, ≥ 32 characters, from a secret store, never logged; changing it voids every token) is required with the port, and the port must differ from `McpPort` (startup refuses otherwise). `AgentPrincipal.IsAgent` now refuses both schemes as a person (Library, Studio). A Warning at start when the port is not one the server listens on. | D077 needed a port that a NetworkPolicy can open to sandboxes without opening the API or the agents' MCP (which honours `X-User-Id` for `actsForUsers` secrets, D084). Binding each scheme to its port makes "headers are ignored" structural instead of a branch someone can forget. |
| D191 | 2026-10-11 | **Sandbox token lifecycle: a signed token plus a live row; revocation is immediate.** Memory issues (`IssueSandboxMemoryTokenCommand`, MediatR from the sandboxes module, which never sees the key) and stores the SHA-256 of the token in `memory_sandbox_tokens` (PK = the sandbox's memory agent id, plus sandbox name, owner, expiry). A token is accepted only if its signature, its `exp` **and** a row with that agent id, that hash and an unexpired `expires_at` all hold, so: **re-issue** (every run that activates a sandbox, and the owner replacing an active one) overwrites the row and kills the previous token; **stop** (`POST …/stop`) revokes the row **before** AX's `DeleteTask` (a revoke that fails fails the stop — a database that does not answer is memory's `Unavailable`, so `502`, never a raw `500`; a failed delete leaves a sandbox without memory, never a stopped one with it; an agent id that is not `ax-<name>-<nonce>`, as on a sandbox made outside Skanyxx, never had a token and is skipped); a re-run that activates a new agent id, or a replace that no longer binds a memory workspace, revokes the previous id first; an owner **disabled or refused by the Entra re-check** (`PrivilegesRevoked` with `AccountDisabled`/`AccessRemoved`) loses every token they own in one local statement that runs before the sandboxes module calls AX (losing supervisor alone revokes nothing). Expiry `Memory:SandboxTokenHours` (default 24, 1–720) bounds a token copied out of a sandbox that nobody stops; a sandbox that runs longer loses memory until its owner re-runs it, or suspends and resumes it (no channel into a running pod: AX v0.3.1 env cannot change without a save). **Resume renews (R2, 2026-10-11):** resuming a sandbox that carries a token issues a new one for the same agent id and owner and saves it into the task's env (still suspended) before AX resumes it, so a sandbox suspended past its expiry keeps memory; memory refusing the renewal refuses the resume. The insert is one `INSERT … ON CONFLICT (agent_id) DO UPDATE … WHERE sandbox_name AND owner_id match`, so an agent id already bound to another sandbox or owner is a `409`, never re-bound; expired rows are swept on each issue. Suspend keeps the token. **Several replicas:** the per-sandbox lock (`KeyedLock`) is in-process, so two replicas replacing or resuming the same sandbox at once can each write a token — the last Postgres writer and the last AX writer win independently, and if they differ the sandbox holds a dead token (401 until its next run or resume). This fails closed and is accepted; a `pg_advisory_xact_lock` around issue + AX save would close it if multi-replica sandboxes become common. Rejected: a revocation list (grows, and fails open on a lost row) and short-expiry renewal (needs a channel into a running sandbox AX does not have). | "Stopping a sandbox makes its token invalid" must hold for a token that left the sandbox (internet egress is opt-in), so expiry alone is not enough; a row keyed by the grants key gives one live token per activation and revocation by delete. |
| D192 | 2026-10-11 | **Whose memory a sandbox has: its own agent id, acting for its owner only.** The token's `agt` (`ax-<name>-<nonce>`, fresh per activation as before) is the caller's agent id, so grants stay `PUT /api/memory/grants/<agent id>` by a supervisor (Sandboxes still never writes grants) and the default (D078) applies without grants: search `company` + the owner's personal scope, upsert the owner's personal scope only. The user is always the token's `own` (the person who ran it) — never `X-User-Id`; `actsForUsers` (D084) does not exist for sandboxes, so no sandbox, whoever wrote its workspace, can name another person. Only ids starting `ax-` get tokens (a token can never carry a kagent/studio agent's grants). **Tokens go only where memory is attached:** a run gets `SKANYXX_MEMORY_URL` + `SKANYXX_MEMORY_TOKEN` only when it binds a workspace whose MCP list has `skanyxx-memory` (a supervisor's `attachMemory`); other runs get none and their previous token is revoked. **The task-image contract (D073):** read the URL from `SKANYXX_MEMORY_URL` (= the workspace's `skanyxx-memory` entry) and send `Authorization: Bearer $SKANYXX_MEMORY_TOKEN` on every MCP request (Streamable HTTP, `initialize` included); `SKANYXX_AGENT_ID`, `SKANYXX_USER_ID`, `SKANYXX_OWNER` are informational. Every `SKANYXX_*` name stays reserved (a caller's is `400`). No agent secret is ever put in a sandbox env (tested). Accepted risk: AX keeps env in plaintext (Redis, task metadata via `atenet-router`), so the token is readable wherever AX's env is — both are fenced from sandboxes (D072, D178), and the token's reach is one owner's personal scope + company search + explicit grants, until stop or expiry. | The threat model of D076 includes workspace writers (supervisors) whose content runs under another owner's identity; fixing the user to the token's owner caps that at the owner the sandbox already acts for. Issuing only with attached memory keeps credentials out of sandboxes that never asked for memory. |

The host part cannot be checked; a Service in front of the listener must use the same port number. `attachMemory` puts this URL on the workspace (D073). No Ingress ever targets it. `deploy/sandboxes/sandbox-egress-memory.yaml` is the hand-applied twin (opt-in in the kustomization). `validate.sh` S1 asserts the Service, ports, env, key source, URL, AllowedHosts and both policies (and their absence without the flag); 6 new refusals (42 total). Template-validated only: not shown from inside a gVisor sandbox on kind (the dev Host runs on the Mac, not in the cluster). | D077: "the only Skanyxx port sandboxes may reach", made a rendered fact instead of an operator instruction. |
| D195 | 2026-10-11 | **The appliance installer as built (D057; todo §4 box 2).** `deploy/appliance/install.sh`, run as root on a fresh Linux machine (amd64/arm64, systemd): installs **k3s `v1.36.5+k3s1`** (the install script of that exact release tag, downloaded to a temp file and run only if its sha256 matches `K3S_INSTALL_SHA256` pinned in install.sh (R2, review A-2) — it then checks the binary's sha256; k3s config `write-kubeconfig-mode: "0600"`, `secrets-encryption: true`) and **Helm `v4.3.0`** into `/opt/skanyxx/bin` (tarball checked against sha256s pinned in the script, never the system's helm), loads the Skanyxx image, then `helm upgrade --install skanyxx` of the umbrella chart into namespace `skanyxx` with `deploy/appliance/values.yaml` + the per-machine settings (host, image, TLS) saved in `/etc/skanyxx/appliance.env` (dir 0700, files 0600), `--wait=watcher`, then waits for `https://<host>/health` 200 **and** for `http://<host>/Setup` to answer 301/308 to `https://<host>/Setup` (R2), and prints the URL and — on the first run only, and only once the token was actually read (a failed read prints a warning and leaves it for the next run or `token`) — the bootstrap token. `--image` must be a plain `repository:tag` reference (checked before it reaches `--set-string`); temp files are removed by an EXIT trap. A re-run is the same upgrade with the same values: the chart's `lookup` keeps every generated Secret (D139), the PVCs (k3s local-path) keep the data. Subcommands `status` (no secret), `token` (the bootstrap token again, root only), `uninstall` (release only; Secrets + PVCs kept, `install` brings it back) and `uninstall --purge [--yes]` (k3s, data, config; typed confirmation). **Image:** `skanyxx-image.tar` next to the script (`docker save`, built by `deploy/appliance/bundle.sh` with a fresh `<UTC time>-<commit>` tag per bundle, since a node never re-reads a tag it holds, D140), imported with `k3s ctr` and copied into k3s's `agent/images` so every k3s start re-imports and pins it; or `--image <registry ref>`. **AX + Substrate stay off** (D069; `ax.enabled: false`, validate.sh checks the render). Shown on an OrbStack Ubuntu 24.04 arm64 machine (task `2026-10-09_1130_skanyxx-fix-partials` W5). | "Appliance = that chart on hidden k3s; they never kubectl" (D057, todo §4). One script, pinned versions, idempotent, nothing to learn. |

The chart's Ingress gets `className: traefik`, TLS from the Secret `skanyxx-tls` (the operator's `--tls-cert/--tls-key`, else a self-signed P-256 certificate for the host, 825 days, regenerated when the host changes or when it expires within 30 days — any install/upgrade run renews it, R2) and two Traefik Middlewares (`deploy/appliance/traefik.yaml`): `https-redirect` (a RedirectScheme, which only acts on a request whose scheme differs, so one router serves both) and `hsts` (`max-age=31536000`; D143 puts HSTS at the ingress). `networkPolicy.ingressNamespaces: [kube-system]` (Traefik's namespace on k3s). The host must be a DNS name (Ingress hosts and `AllowedHosts` cannot be an IP): `--host`, else `hostname -f` when it has a dot. | Outside Development the Host sets Secure cookies, so plain http cannot sign anyone in; Traefik is already on every k3s and its Middlewares are not the nginx annotations the chart refuses. |

| D198 | 2026-10-11 | **The k3s API (6443), the kubelet (10250) and flannel's VXLAN (8472/udp) answer only the machine and its pods.** `/opt/skanyxx/bin/skanyxx-firewall` adds the chain `SKANYXX-FW` to INPUT (iptables + ip6tables; the system's binaries, else the ones k3s ships): RETURN for `lo` and `cni0`, DROP for those ports from any other interface. It runs as an `ExecStartPre` drop-in of `k3s.service` on every start and right after install. **Failures are visible (R2, review A-5):** the drop-in has no `-` prefix, so a firewall that cannot be applied fails the k3s start (fail closed; `systemctl status k3s` says why); the script exits 0 in exactly one case, iptables missing because k3s's very first start has not unpacked it yet, and install.sh applies the rules right after that start (so for those seconds 6443/10250 listen unfiltered — authentication still applies). `install.sh status` reports, per family, the jump's position in INPUT and the DROP rule count (2), and warns when a non-`KUBE-*` rule sits above the jump: k3s inserts its own jumps above it on every start, which is fine, but a ufw/firewalld (or hand-made) ACCEPT above it would let those ports through before SKANYXX-FW sees them. kube-proxy's healthz (10256) stays reachable (status only). Not `--bind-address 127.0.0.1`: the in-cluster `kubernetes` Service points at the node's address, and kagent's controller needs the API. Shown: from the Mac both machine addresses time out on 6443/10250 (before and after a machine restart) while 80/443 answer. | "k3s API bound to localhost or firewalled": nobody outside the box needs Kubernetes, and an exposed VXLAN port lets anyone on the LAN inject packets into the pod network. |

| D201 | 2026-10-11 | **Remote mode contract.** `Skanyxx:Mode` (`Server` \| `Remote`, case-insensitive; absent/empty = `Server`; anything else exits 1 with a Fatal line) and `Skanyxx:RemoteUrl` are read by the Host (`HostModes`, `RemoteClient`) before any module or database loads, and are **not** in Core's `SkanyxxOptions`: modules never see them. Remote builds with Serilog only — no `AppDbContext`/`skanyxx.db`, no `ModuleLoader`, no MediatR, no Identity/authentication, no Swagger; the `AllowedHosts` guard still applies. `GET`/`HEAD /health` → 200 `{"mode":"remote"}`; any other `GET`/`HEAD` → **302 to RemoteUrl as configured**, never the request's path or query (no open-redirect surface); every other method → 404. RemoteUrl: absolute, `https://` (or `http://` to a loopback host — the chart's `publicUrl` rule, D145), no whitespace, backslashes, query, fragment or user info, else exit 1. Empty RemoteUrl: `GET /` → 200 page naming the config file and key, everything else 404. Installers: macOS/Linux sed `"Mode": "Server"` → `"Remote"` on a fresh config with the same verify-or-remove rule as the bootstrap token; Windows asks for the address on a wizard page (or `/REMOTEURL=`) and writes both keys into the fresh `appsettings.json`. | The smallest client that keeps company data off the laptop. Path forwarding would make a local open redirect and couple the launcher to the server's routes; a fixed target needs neither. |

| D214 | 2026-10-20 | **Sources (P9, as built).** Git: an https archive of a 40-hex SHA — github.com via codeload, other hosts only from `Plugins:GitHosts` (`gitea` → `/archive/<sha>.zip`, `gitlab` → `/-/archive/<sha>/…`); ≤ 20 MB (`Plugins:MaxDownloadBytes`), 30 s, ≤ 3 redirects on the same host, no proxy, and a ConnectCallback that resolves once and connects only to the addresses it checked (loopback, private, link-local, ULA, CGNAT, NAT64/6to4-embedded private refused unless `Plugins:AllowPrivateNetworks`). Zip: ≤ 10 MB upload, ≤ 50 MB expanded (counted while decompressing), ≤ 2 000 entries, files over 1 MB skipped, absolute paths / `..` / `\` / `:` / symlinks refuse the whole zip; a single wrapping folder is stripped unless it is a layout folder. Values nothing can deploy are deleted: at once when a plugin proposal is closed in Skanyxx, and by the reconcile pass for unclaimed records named by neither main nor an open plugin proposal once older than an hour (an install stores values just before its proposal opens). Outside Development without `Identity:DataProtectionCertificatePath` the agents module warns at startup and `/Plugins` shows the owner that the key ring is in clear. | D225 | 2026-10-20 | **Plugin servers and agents (plugin review round 2).** An agent no plugin owns uses plugin servers of **at most one plugin**, and never together with an allow-listed `Studio:McpServers` server, memory grants or kagent TTL memory (`DraftRules`, which now takes server → plugin): checked on the form (propose, edit), at inspection and merge, at install and on every reconcile pass. The reconciler also holds a plugin's own agents to the recipe rules (`PluginRules.CheckRecipe`: no grants, no TTL, no skills, own servers only), and an agent more than one plugin manifest lists is invalid, logged and never applied. An install whose new version moves a server (another address, D226) stores nothing for it: the values the running server has stay untouched until the move is merged, the report says the owner enters the values after merge (naming the headers), and the review's "moves to" note still fires; memory refuses an install's store (`KeepExisting`) over values held for another address (`409 moved`). | D226 | 2026-10-20 | **Plugin server addresses (plugin review round 2).** Header values are bound to the server URL's **scheme, host, port and path** (the URL without query; `PluginServerUrl.Address`), not the authority: multi-tenant hosts (Smithery, Composio, Zapier) tell tenants apart by path. The column is `value_address` (`character varying(2048)`), and the plugin-server table is created by **one** migration, `PluginServers` (generated with dotnet-ef; the two unshipped migrations `PluginServers` + `PluginServerAuthority` of round 1 are folded into it; this supersedes D220's `value_authority` / `PluginServerAuthority`). URL rules added: a **fragment** is refused; the host must be **plain ASCII**, checked on the URL as written before .NET maps it, whatever `Plugins:AllowPrivateServers` says; a trailing dot is dropped before the IP test; the last label must be two or more letters (else internal). Query keys are judged on whole words (split on `_ - .` and camelCase; `apikey`, `accesstoken` whole), so `author`/`design`/`barcode` pass and `api_key`/`apiKey`/`access-token`/`X-Sig` are refused. The header denylist adds `True-Client-IP`, `X-Client-IP`, `CF-Connecting-IP`, `X-Original-Forwarded-For`, `X-Original-URL`, `X-Rewrite-URL`, `X-Host`, `X-HTTP-Method-Override` (with `X-Real-IP`). | A token entered for one tenant's path must not follow a rename to another tenant on the same host. Full-width and trailing-dot hosts slipped past the internal-name check; a fragment is written to git; a substring match refused ordinary keys. |
| D227 | 2026-10-20 | **Purging stored plugin values (plugin review round 2).** The pass lists the open proposals before it lists main again for the purge, so a proposal merged mid-pass is in main by then, never in neither. A record is kept only when main or an open plugin proposal gives its server to **the record's plugin**; a row another plugin left (its proposal closed in git) is purged once older than `PurgeAfter`, so it never blocks the server's new plugin. The delete is conditional on the plugin and on `updated_at` still being older than the cut-off (`RemovePluginServerCommand(Plugin, UpdatedBefore)`), and every store renews `updated_at`, changed or not, so a reinstall's proposal is covered by the grace period. Inspection and uninstall find who uses a leaving server from each other agent's `agent.yaml` only (one read per folder, valid or not) and log nothing; only the pass logs invalid folders at Error (`ReadMainAsync(…, log)`). | A merge landing between the pass's two git reads lost the plugin's literal values; another plugin's leftover row made a server unconfigurable; request paths did two reads per agent and logged at Error. |
| D228 | 2026-10-20 | **Accepted risks (plugin review round 2).** (1) The head of a git or zip plugin proposal is not re-fetched or bound to what was inspected and installed: the owner-only merge of git/zip proposals (D222), with Gitea writable by Skanyxx's account only (D110, D112), is the control. (2) Stored values are purged at once only when the proposal is closed through Skanyxx; one closed in Gitea directly is purged by the pass once the record is older than `PurgeAfter` (1 h), and an open proposal of the same plugin keeps its server's values alive. (3) The internal-name check does not refuse a two-label cluster name such as `gitea.skanyxx` or `kubernetes.default`, nor a public name resolving inward: kagent's egress NetworkPolicy is the control. The chart fences kagent's agent pods (internet minus `networkPolicy.clusterCidrs`, port 443, plus `modelEgress.extra`) but leaves kagent's controller, which discovers tools with the header values, open (`egress: [{}]`), and renders nothing for a BYO kagent; the README says so. (4) A database that applied round 1's two plugin migrations (a developer's) must drop `memory_studio_plugin_servers` and their two `__EFMigrationsHistory` rows before the folded migration applies; no shipped database has them. | Each is either covered by a stronger control or confined to an unshipped state; closing them costs more than the risk left. |
| D229 | 2026-10-21 | **kagent's controller egress (plugin review round 3, sec3 R4).** kagent's controller opens an MCP session to every plugin server for tool discovery, sending the header values from its Secret, and Skanyxx cannot fence that connection itself (kagent resolves and connects, and DNS can rebind). The chart adds `networkPolicy.controllerEgress` (bundled kagent only): DNS (default-deny), `kubeApiCidrs` on `kubeApiPorts`, the bundled Postgres, Skanyxx's mcp port, the agent pods on `a2aPorts`, the internet minus `clusterCidrs` on 443, and `extra`. **Default off**, because the Kubernetes API's endpoint addresses differ per cluster and cannot be guessed: a default-on policy would either cut the controller off the API (it stops reconciling) or need a guessed range wide enough to be no fence. The open default is no longer silent: the chart refuses `controllerEgress.enabled` without `kubeApiCidrs`, and with BYO kagent (where it would fence nothing); `scripts/helm/validate.sh` renders it and checks each rule. In the app, outside Development, a startup warning names `Plugins:AllowHttp` and `Plugins:AllowPrivateServers` when on (each lets the controller send header values to an in-cluster address), and `/Plugins` tells the owner the controller is unfenced unless this policy is on. This completes D228(3)'s acceptance. |
| D230 | 2026-10-21 | **Plugin rules, round 3.** (1) A server URL whose path has a `.` or `..` segment, raw or percent-encoded in any case, is refused: .NET collapses dot segments while kagent sends the path as written, so the address values are bound to (D226) would not be the one asked for (sec3 R2). (2) Credential query keys also match a word ending in `key`, `token`, `secret`, `password`, `passwd`, `signature` or `credential(s)` (`authtoken`, `clientsecret`), and every query value gets the random-string test path segments get; `monkey`-style keys are refused too, which only over-refuses (sec3 R3). (3) A last label in `xn--` form is a public top-level domain (cr3#6). (4) A server is a plugin's own only when its file is in that plugin's folder: a `plugin.yaml` listing another folder's server is invalid, and own servers are filtered by folder for the recipe check too, so a hand edit of main cannot pass the one-plugin rule (sec3 R1). (5) Install and memory treat a server as moved only when its row is claimed: an unclaimed row at another address runs nowhere and is replaced (cr3#4). (6) A close takes its time before closing and purges only records older than that, catching any non-cancellation failure as best effort (cr3#2, #3). (7) Agent folders the strict reader refuses still count as users of the servers their `agent.yaml` names (cr3#1). (8) `X-Original-Host` and `X-Envoy-*` join the header denylist; the install report notes a plugin shipping servers without headers beside credentialed ones (sec3 R5). |
| D231 | 2026-10-26 | **Desktop installers, release round (diagnostic 2026-10-26).** (1) Every launcher starts the Host in its install folder — the macOS `/usr/local/bin/skanyxx` is a `cd … && exec` script (no longer a symlink; `/usr/local/bin` created), Linux ships `/usr/bin/skanyxx` and `/usr/share/skanyxx/open-in-browser` (the desktop entry) — because the Host reads `appsettings.json` and `modules/` from its working directory: run elsewhere it crashed at the AllowedHosts guard, and a folder holding a `modules/` DLL would have run it. (2) A fresh install (Remote mode) gets no BootstrapToken and a `0644 root` config: Remote mode never uses the token, and the CLI and desktop entry run as the user (`0600 root` / `0640 root:admin` locked them out). Upgraded configs are never edited. (3) Linux: `prerm` stops/disables only on `remove`, `postinst` try-restarts on upgrade, `postrm purge` removes `/usr/share/skanyxx`, `libicu72` (Debian 12) joins Depends, Installed-Size is measured, `.so` files are 0644; the unit stays root (upgraded Server-mode configs are `0600 root`) but read-only except its folder, no home, no new privileges, start limit 5 per 2 min. (4) macOS: `preinstall` empties `modules/`, `preremove` (never run by a pkg) is gone, the LaunchAgent logs nowhere in `/tmp`, `hostArchitectures` is the build's own, uninstall adds `pkgutil --forget`; Windows: `[InstallDelete]` empties `modules\`, `open-browser.cmd` is removed, upgrades close a running Host, a dev `appsettings.json` is never packaged. (5) `release.yml`: tag/title reach the shell only through env and the tag must match `vX.Y.Z[-pre]`; the tag must be (or be created on) the built commit and an existing release is never added to; `contents: read` except the release job; no persisted credentials; actions pinned to SHAs; `gh release create` replaces the third-party action; deb versions map `-` to `~`; module copies fail unless every module is there; `SHA256SUMS` + build provenance; optional Authenticode, Developer ID signing and notarization from secrets. | Found by running the installers (evidence: task `2026-10-26_1941_skanyxx-release-diagnostic`): the documented start command crashed on macOS and Linux, a non-root user could not read the config, and `dpkg -i` left the app without ICU. |

(5) The unit keeps root's `~/.kube`, `~/.aws`, `~/.azure` (Server mode runs kubectl/aws/az) through `ProtectHome=tmpfs` + `BindPaths`, adds `ProtectProc=invisible`, `UMask=0077`. (6) Windows empties `modules\` only on an upgrade and asks before closing a running Host. | D330 | 2026-10-29 | **A form body over the request cap is a 413, as a JSON body is (Chat upload flake).** Kestrel refuses a body whose declared length is over the endpoint's `RequestSizeLimit` with a `BadHttpRequestException` (413) on the first read, before any of it is buffered. A **workflow** is a name, a description, an optional team it is shared with, and a tree of steps: an agent step names a merged kagent agent (`namespace/name`, the agents Chat offers: `skanyxx.dev/merged=true`, not suspended) and a prompt template with `{{input}}` and `{{steps.<id>}}`; a group runs its steps `sequential` or `parallel`; top-level steps run one after another. Caps: `Investigate:MaxDepth` 4 levels, `MaxSteps` 40 nodes (groups count), `MaxPromptChars` 8 000, `MaxInputChars` 20 000. A prompt may only read a step that has finished by the time it runs (an earlier step of a sequence, never a parallel sibling or a later step): checked on save, with a message per problem. Agents are checked against the merged list on save and again on start (409 `agent-unavailable`; kagent down 502). `/Investigate` (nav item already in NavCatalog) has a Workflows tab (cards: Run, Edit/View steps, Duplicate, History, Delete) with a button-driven tree editor (add agent step or group, move up/down, remove, agent dropdown), a run panel with live per-step status, timings and answers (polled every 1.5 s while queued or running), Cancel, Run again, Report (PDF/JSON) and the memory card's link, and a History tab with workflow, status, name and "only mine" filters; design system only, its own `investigate.css` of tokens, the `investigate` setup guide. Handlers use MediatR, the solution's mediator (every module and every cross-module request is MediatR; a second mediator would split the pipeline and its validation behaviour). | Investigations are multi-agent work people want to repeat; a tree of sequential and parallel steps is the smallest model that covers "ask three agents at once, then summarise". |

| D342 | 2026-10-29 | **How a run executes.** Start copies the workflow's steps into the run with one pending row per step (`investigate_step_runs`, its own row per step so parallel steps never write the same one) and wakes `InvestigationWorker`, a `BackgroundService` that claims queued runs oldest first by one conditional update (`queued` → `running`), so with several replicas each run executes once, up to `MaxConcurrentRuns` (4) per replica. Caps on start, counted under advisory lock `(0x494E5601, 0)`: `MaxActiveRunsPerUser` 3, `MaxActiveRuns` 20 (429). Each agent step is one blocking A2A `message/send` (`X-User-Id: skanyxx-investigate`) under its own deadline (`StepTimeoutSeconds`, 300; HttpClient's own timeout is infinite so it never cuts first); groups run their steps in order or with `Task.WhenAll`. The first failed or timed-out step fails the run with a readable reason ("Step 'Lookup' (kagent/researcher) failed: timed out after 300 s.") and cancels the run's token, so parallel siblings end `cancelled` and steps never reached end `skipped`. Cancel is asked through the database (`cancel_requested`; a queued run is cancelled at once, a running one within `WatchMilliseconds`), so any replica's API can stop any run; the same watcher stamps a heartbeat, and a running run whose heartbeat is older than `StaleAfterSeconds` (its replica died) is failed. On shutdown every executing run is cancelled, awaited and ends failed ("Skanyxx stopped"), never left running. State writes after a cancel run on `CancellationToken.None`. Answers are cut at `MaxOutputChars`, a rendered prompt at `MaxRenderedPromptChars`, each saying so. The A2A call carries what a person typed and earlier answers, so it follows D322 as a secret-carrying call: a refusal logs method, path, status and `SecretCallRefused`, never kagent's body, and the run's reason is a fixed category. | The run must survive many agents, slow models, a person changing their mind and a replica dying, without paying for a step twice or leaving a run "running" forever. |

The run keeps the card's scope and key; the run panel links to it in the Library and the JSON report names it. Failed and cancelled runs leave no card: there is no finding. Chat's no-op extractor answers null for it. | The finding is company knowledge once someone lifts it; the run stays the evidence it points back to. |

| D373 | 2026-10-29 | **Investigate engine hardening (QA round 1 minors).** A step whose row cannot be written (or any internal error in a step) fails the run at once and cancels its siblings. The worker claims one run at a time and starts it at once, so a database error claiming the next never strands a claimed run. Heartbeats, claims and the stale sweep use the database's clock (`DateTime.UtcNow` translated to `now()`); the sweep ends the run first, by a conditional update, then its steps in the same transaction (never started skipped, running cancelled). An `HttpRequestException` after the call was cancelled is a cancellation, not an agent failure. `Investigate:MaxParallelSteps` (4) bounds a run's concurrent agent calls (a slot per agent step, never held by a group); `Investigate:MaxWorkflowsPerUser` (100) caps create and duplicate (409 `too-many`); the run list is projected to its summary in SQL. Step answers, step errors and run failures pass through `SecretText.Redact` before they are stored, exported or read by a later step. | Review findings: siblings ran to their timeouts, replicas' clocks disagreed, and a 40-step parallel workflow could fire 40 calls at once. |

Handlers: team and department limits are the owner's; a supervisor sets personal limits only for members of their own teams, never their own, and never above that person's tightest team or department limit; a supervisor decides only their members' requests, never their own, and approves only while the person's effective limit plus the amount stays within that cap. The check runs before a turn, so a turn that starts under the limit may finish over it (percent can pass 100). Usage is one atomic `INSERT … ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE` per turn; recording is bounded to 2 s and a failure is logged, never thrown into the chat. A database failure on the pre-turn check fails the turn loudly. | Never block on missing data silently: an unreported turn still costs something, and saying it is an estimate is honest. | D338 | 2026-10-29 | **Quota RBAC and counting, after review (refines D333, D334).** (1) A supervisor's cap is checked on the person's *resulting effective limit*, approved extras included: setting or clearing a personal limit is refused when it would end above the tightest team or department limit, unless it only lowers the effective limit (so a supervisor can always tighten). Approve-then-raise and approve-then-clear are refused. Clearing within the cap stays allowed, even over an owner-set personal limit. (2) A supervisor never acts on the owner or on another supervisor, even in a shared team; the overview names whom the viewer may act for (`actFor`) and shows a supervisor only those people's requests, so a peer's or the owner's reason is not readable. (3) A turn's count is kagent's total: when the total is above prompt plus candidates (thinking and tool-use prompt tokens), the remainder counts as completion. (4) The supervisor cap is checked on a snapshot and written separately, so two supervisors acting at the same moment can overshoot by one action; that is accepted, bounded, and only open to authorised supervisors. (5) Studio preview turns (`StudioChat`) talk to kagent directly and are not checked or counted: quotas cover Chat only for now. | Without (1) the cap was one extra step from meaningless; without (2) a supervisor could lock the owner out (limit 0); without (3) thinking models would be undercounted by a large factor. |
| D339 | 2026-10-29 | **A supervisor's cap without a team or department limit (QA round 1, security M1; refines D333, D338 (1)).** The cap is the person's tightest team or department limit; with none, their **personal limit as it stands** (`QuotaResolver.SupervisorCap`); with neither, there is no cap, because the person is unlimited and no supervisor action can give them more than they have (a supervisor may then set a first limit). Under a personal-limit cap a supervisor may only lower it: raising or clearing it is refused, and so is approving any request (the effective limit plus the amount would pass it); the owner does both. Once lowered, the lower value is the cap. D338's "clearing within the cap stays allowed" holds only under a team or department cap. | Without it, an owner's personal limit on someone in a team with no team budget was one supervisor click from unlimited. Using the limit as it stands, rather than tracking who set it, needs no new column and can only err on the strict side. |

Blocking `POST api/chat` now records on failure, timeout and when the caller leaves; a stopped or failed stream records the same way. Recording comes first, then the kagent cancel and the session cleanup, so a cleanup that throws loses nothing; each turn records exactly once (`TurnStart.FirstRecord`). A streamed message carrying usage counts once per `messageId` (kagent can repeat a message). The blocking path uses the same total rule as the stream: whatever `totalTokenCount` holds beyond the prompt is output (`A2AUsage`). Not done: history and images are not in the estimate (it is a floor, and marked as one); a failed database write is still only logged (D334). | A stopped or failed turn was free, which is an easy way around a limit; a repeated final message doubled a turn. |

A place nobody returns frees itself after 15 minutes. So the overshoot is at most 2 turns per replica (about 2 × replicas × the largest turn); people without a limit are never held back. `IChatQuota.RefusalAsync` is replaced by `AdmitAsync`. | Reserving an estimate in the database would need a reservation table and its cleanup; a per-person in-flight count gives a known bound with no new state. |

| D521 | 2026-10-29 | **Skanyxx signs every kagent call with a short-lived HS256 JWT (`KAgent:Auth:Mode=signed`, `TokenKey`, `TokenSeconds` 10-300, default 60).** Claims: `iss=skanyxx`, `aud=kagent-gate`, `sub`, `iat`, `exp`, `jti`. `sub` is the user the call names, read the way kagent reads it: `?user_id=`, else `X-User-Id`, else `skanyxx`. `KAgentTokenHandler` sits on every HttpClient through `ConfigureHttpClientDefaults` (Investigate's and Tickets' own kagent clients included) and acts on kagent's origin only. It strips `user_id` and `X-User-Id`, so no module changed. On 8084 Envoy requires the token: 401/403 when it is missing, bad, `alg none`, expired, without `exp`, longer than 300 s, or for another issuer or audience. It drops `X-User-Id` and `X-Agent-Name` and sets `X-User-Id` from `sub`. It does not forward the token. It refuses (400) a `user_id` query key in any case, any query key holding a `%` escape (Go decodes `user%5Fid`; found by `gate-test.sh`), and header names with `_`. `/health` is anonymous. The key is generated once into the Secret `kagent-gate` (`token-key`, plus `jwks.json` for Envoy, the same bytes base64url) and kept; `existingSecret` for GitOps. The local default is `Mode=none` (explicit in `appsettings.template.json`); `signed` without a 32+ character key stops startup. | A bare `X-User-Id` proves nothing; a token only Skanyxx can sign, naming the person and dying within a minute, does, and the gate makes it the only identity kagent sees. |

The agents' own `X-User-Id` is passed on, since agents name the user of their session. | Agents had the whole controller API as any user. The calls they need are known from kagent's ADK source.
